Scanning the sequence reveals no &quot;GPD&quot; triad, which strongly argues against an AP lyase-containing glycosylase.<br><br>2. **Absence of zinc finger:** Fpg (the canonical protein for option D) contains a conserved zinc-binding Cys₃/His₁ motif. The catalytic histidine (for protonating uracil N3) appears to be present in the context of &quot;HFLR.&quot;<br><br>**Functional Interpretation:**<br>- UNG catalyzes hydrolytic cleavage of the glycosidic bond of uracil from DNA (producing an abasic site)<br>- It is *monofunctional* (lacks AP lyase activity), which is consistent with the absence of GPD motif and N-terminal amine positioning for β-elimination chemistry<br>- The resulting abasic site is then processed by AP endonucleases (Xth/Nfo or Ape1) to initiate base excision repair (BER)<br><br>This matches option A perfectly.<br><br>answer: A</td>

<td valign="top"><strong>Question:</strong> Which condition is associated with induction of this protein?<br><br><strong>Choices:</strong><br><br><strong>A.</strong> By tryptophan-activated TRAP, through TRAP-dependent transcriptional regulation<br><br><strong>B.</strong> By uncharged Trp tRNA, via the T-box transcription antitermination mechanism<br><br><strong>C.</strong> By charged Trp tRNA, via the T-box transcription antitermination mechanism<br><br><strong>D.</strong> By uncharged Trp tRNA, via stringent-response transcriptional activation<br><br><strong>Flash-Next response:</strong><br><br><br><br>1. reasoning: <br><br>**Analyzing the protein sequence:** The sequence is very short (57 amino acids), suggesting it may be a small component of a bacterial operon involved in amino acid biosynthesis.<br><br>**Biological context:** All four choices relate to tryptophan (Trp) regulation in bacteria, so this protein is part of a tryptophan-related operon.<br><br>**Evaluating each option:**<br><br>- **Option A (TRAP-dependent regulation):** TRAP (tryptophan RNA-binding Attenuation Protein) is activated BY tryptophan (charged state) and then REPRESSES transcription by promoting terminator formation. This describes repression, not induction of biosynthetic genes.<br><br>- **Option B (T-box, uncharged Trp-tRNA):** The T-box mechanism works as follows: when tryptophan is scarce → uncharged Trp-tRNA accumulates → uncharged tRNA binds the T-box leader RNA via codon-anticodon interaction → stabilizes the antiterminator structure → prevents terminator stem-loop → RNA polymerase reads through → biosynthetic genes are expressed. This correctly describes INDUCTION when tryptophan is limiting.<br><br>- **Option C (T-box, charged Trp-tRNA):** When Trp is abundant, charged tRNA cannot bind the T-box properly, the terminator forms, and transcription is terminated. This describes the NON-induced state.<br><br>- **Option D (Stringent response):** The stringent response is mediated by (p)ppGpp alarmones, not directly by uncharged tRNA binding to leader RNA.<br><br>**Conclusion:** The T-box transcription antitermination mechanism is induced by uncharged Trp-tRNA, which signals tryptophan starvation and allows read-through of the attenuator to express downstream biosynthetic genes.<br><br>2. answer: B</td>
<td valign="top"><strong>Question:</strong> Which specific regulatory input–mechanism pair is associated with induction of this protein, rather than merely placing it in a broad amino-acid-response category?<br><br><strong>Choices:</strong><br><br><strong>A.</strong> Tryptophan-activated TRAP, acting through TRAP-dependent transcriptional regulation<br><br><strong>B.</strong> Uncharged tryptophan tRNA, acting through T-box transcription antitermination<br><br><strong>C.</strong> Charged tryptophan tRNA, acting through T-box transcription antitermination<br><br><strong>D.</strong> Uncharged tryptophan tRNA, acting through stringent-response transcriptional activation<br><br><strong>Skills:</strong> GO_biological_process_0014:2<br><br><strong>Flash-Next response:</strong><br><br><br><br>1. reasoning: <br><br>Let me analyze this step by step.<br><br>**Protein sequence analysis:** The sequence is short (~60 amino acids) with a distinctive cysteine-rich pattern (CKCE...CPACS), suggesting it could be a small regulatory or structural protein potentially involved in tryptophan metabolism or transport.<br><br>**Evaluating each option:**<br><br>- **Option A (TRAP):** TRAP (Trp RNA-binding Attenuation Protein) is activated BY tryptophan itself, and when activated, it REPRESSES (causes transcription termination of) target genes. This is a repression mechanism, not an induction mechanism. Additionally, the input is tryptophan directly, not tRNA.<br><br>- **Option B (Uncharged Trp-tRNA + T-box antitermination):** T-box riboswitches are well-characterized RNA elements in the 5&#x27; UTR of genes in Gram-positive bacteria. They specifically sense the **uncharged** (deacylated) acceptor end of the cognate tRNA. When the amino acid is scarce, uncharged tRNA accumulates, binds the T-box, causes **antitermination** of an intrinsic terminator, and thus **induces** transcription of the downstream gene. This is a specific input (uncharged tRNA) + mechanism (antitermination) pair that drives induction.<br><br>- **Option C (Charged Trp-tRNA + T-box antitermination):** This is biologically incorrect. Charged tRNA cannot bind the T-box aptamer domain.<br><br>- **Option D (Uncharged Trp-tRNA + stringent response):** While uncharged tRNA can trigger the stringent response via RelA, the stringent response is a global regulatory response mediated by (p)ppGpp, not a specific gene-level induction mechanism. It also doesn&#x27;t fit the &quot;specific input–mechanism pair&quot; criterion the question emphasizes.<br><br>**Conclusion:** The question asks for a *specific* regulatory input–mechanism pair that *induces* the protein. Option B correctly identifies uncharged tryptophan tRNA as the sensing input and T-box transcription antitermination as the specific mechanism enabling induction when tryptophan is scarce.<br><br>2. answer: B</td>
